# Atlas — V1 Roadmap

> **How to read this file:** every technical term has a plain-English
> analogy in parentheses so a non-coder owner can follow along. This is a
> **living document** — milestones, scope, and priorities will shift as
> we build. Edit freely.

> **May 2026 pivot note.** This roadmap was reset after the product
> pivoted from "editorial city guide" to "creator platform for audio
> tours." See `atlas_claude_code_prompt.md` for the spec. The earlier
> editorial-reader milestones (M4–M11 in the previous draft) are gone
> because the product shape that needed them is gone. The previous
> roadmap survives in git history if anyone needs to look back.

---

## Owner direction (May 2026)

Principles that override everything else in this file:

1. **Functionality first; design and tone deferred.** Don't burn
   cycles on color palettes, typography, app icons, custom map pins,
   or final editorial tone. Get the audio-tour experience working end
   to end, then polish.
2. **Build so the deferred design pass is cheap.** All new code uses
   `Theme/Atlas{Colors,Typography,Spacing}.swift` tokens even though
   their values are placeholders. The future design decision should be
   a 3-file change, not a 60-file rewrite.
3. **V1 is consumer-side only, backend-free.** Per the spec, payments,
   accounts, and in-app maker upload are post-V1. The V1 UI is built
   in the *shape* of the eventual creator platform so nothing the
   consumer sees needs to be redesigned later.
4. **Review workflow.** Owner reviews via iOS Simulator or TestFlight,
   not by reading code. Each milestone ends in a runnable, reviewable
   state.

**Status (2026-09-03, session 141, branch `claude/bottom-module-missing-45z6ep`):** web session (**code**) — **the bottom module went missing again, and it was HIDDEN rather than uninstalled.** A tester's screenshot: no mini-player, no tab bar, on a **settled Home tab** with the drawer correctly reserving its 126pt and map showing through. 🔴 **Three previous fixes all asked whether the secondary window INSTALLED; this one installs fine and is then left hidden.** The launch hides it on purpose (it sits at `.normal + 1` and would paint over the splash) — but the hide was **latched** off `launchState.isSplashVisible`, against a single unhide in `runLaunchGate` that fires **32 ms before that turns false**, across a guaranteed suspension point. Any `installBottomModule()` in that gap re-hid the window **with nothing left to undo it**, and **`scenePhase == .active` calls it unguarded** on a cold launch — bars gone for the session, every tab, no way back but a force-quit. 🔴 **And the #443 inline fallback could not catch it**: it asked `!isInstalled` and the window **was** installed, so neither surface drew the bars. ✅ **The screenshot ruled out the obvious theory before any code was read** — the search bar and drawer ride the **same `handOffProgress`** the module does and were both settled, so it was not merely parked off-screen at opacity 0. **Fix:** the App owns `launchHoldsBottomModule`, dropped in the **same main-actor turn** as the unhide, and `installBottomModule()` **re-derives** visibility rather than commanding a hide — so a mistimed call can only reach the right answer and, because that site runs on **every foreground, a background round-trip becomes a guaranteed recovery**; the fallback switches to **`isShowingBars`** (installed AND not hidden); and `setHidden` writes the window on **every** call so a drifted state self-corrects. Both rules are pure statics beside `installOutcome`; **7 new tests, 15 total.** ⚠️ **Deliberately NOT fixed and stated rather than buried:** a stuck `appShared.hidesBottomModule` re-derives *from* the stuck flag and cannot heal — left to `LinkEmbedView`'s three restore paths rather than adding a rule that could fight the tour wizard. ⚠️ `ContentView.body` now depends on the launch **phase** (twice a launch), **not** `handOffProgress` (60 Hz, still leaves-only — session 102 intact). **CI green** (validator + iOS Simulator build + unit tests); ⚠️ **nothing compiled locally**, so **the device check was the real proof** — and it passed: **merged as [#728](https://github.com/ehky2882/TRAVEL-GUIDED-TOUR/pull/728) (squash `4da52445`) after the owner verified TestFlight 1.1.2 (141)**. ⚠️ **That build cost a version bump:** build 140 compiled and signed cleanly and was rejected at the upload step on **90186 *"Invalid Pre-Release Train. The train version '1.1.1' is closed"*** — so **Apple has approved 1.1.1** and `MARKETING_VERSION` moved to **1.1.2**; in a web session, with no App Store Connect key, that rejection is the only signal a version shipped. Detail: `archive/HANDOFF-260903-4.md`. Earlier status follows.

**Status (2026-08-31, session 129b):** web session (**tooling**) — **the two checks the Natural History Museum fix said were missing now exist.** Owner: *"add the two missing checks"*. **🔴 The byte checker was blind to this by construction, not by oversight:** everything `check-image-duplicates.py` did before today is about two DIFFERENT urls holding the same bytes (the Thyssen bug), while the NHM case is two entries pointing at the SAME url — and **one file hashed once is one file, so it never forms a group at all**; nothing anywhere touched `audioURL`. this is the door back to the one map that holds everything. **The landing is the one Search already writes**, deliberately, so the map never looks different depending on which door you came through. **Owner decisions taken before any code:** a creator or list page raises **no card** (many tours, many cities, no single subject — it frames the lot, the picture their own `SHOW ALL` gives), and a place raises **its own place card**, not a member tour's (a site with ≥2 tours draws as ONE capsule pin). **🔴 Setting state is not enough** — every surface with the button sits inside a UIKit layer that covers the main window, and SwiftUI can stop delivering updates to a covered hierarchy, so `MapExpander.performExpand` tears the layers down directly (the `performDismiss` rule; the dead tab bar of session 74 and the dead place pin of #532); **the move is then delivered on the next main-queue turn**, and `HomeView` consumes it from three triggers including `isActive` becoming true. **⚠️ One component for all four surfaces** — this app has paid three times for a map idiom copied per surface. **🔴 It is TOP-trailing, and the bottom corner is a bug this shipped once:** the owner reported *"dozent page map expansion doesnt seem to work"*, because these maps sit inside a scrolling page while the mini-player and tab bar render in a window **above** it, and the creator page's header pushes the square map down far enough that its bottom third is under the bar **at the resting scroll position** — a control that renders, sits in the accessibility tree, and is hit-tested by the tab bar (the `UIPageControl` family of #571). ⚠️ **My own first pass called that a harness artifact**, having scrolled the map into view before tapping — *a tap that only works after you scroll the control somewhere convenient is not a passing test.* **⚠️ The expand region is deliberately NOT the region the inline map frames**, and `MapExpander.regionFraming` takes the **pin** coordinate (stop 0 for a walk), never the centroid. **Verification:** `test_sim` **578/578** (+8), and driven end to end in the simulator on tour detail, a place and a creator page; the list page shares `TourSetMap`. Code only — no SQL, no catalogue change. Shipped as [#671](https://github.com/ehky2882/TRAVEL-GUIDED-TOUR/pull/671) (squash `01c70f63`), **merged** on **TestFlight 1.1.1 (138)** after owner device review — *"LOOKS GOOD."* See `archive/HANDOFF-260830-8.md`. Earlier status follows.

**Status (2026-08-30, session 122c):** web session (**tooling**) — **the duplicate-image checker had never seen a link pin.** Not "could not scope to a batch," which is what six sessions in a row recorded: `scripts/check-image-duplicates.py` iterates `catalog["tours"]` and nothing else, so **all 244 link-pin heroes were invisible to it, `--all` included**, from the day [#597](https://github.com/ehky2882/TRAVEL-GUIDED-TOUR/pull/597) split them into a sibling `linkPins` array on 25 August — six days after the script was written. Measured before the fix: **`--all` saw 5,595 images and 0 of the 240 pin heroes**, reporting success over a set it was not fully checking. ⚠️ **My own first description of this to the owner was wrong** — I called it a convenience gap and said nothing in the catalogue was wrong because of it; the catalogue *is* clean, but that is now a measurement (**5,835 images, 0 errors, 27 INFO, 0 fetch failures**), not an assumption, and it is the same shape as the SSL failure that once made this script print "OK" having fetched nothing. **Adds `--pins` and makes `--all` honest; `--maker <CODE>` stays tours-only deliberately**, because pinned creators' handles collide with city codes as substrings (**`STO` matches `@urbanstoriesyt`, `@hollywoodhistory` and four more; 31 collisions catalogue-wide**), so widening it would drag unrelated creators into a city check. ⚠️ **A link pin carries no audio and `tour_slug` derives from `audioURL`** — every pin's is `""`, so all 244 collapsed onto one empty slug until the hero-filename fallback. Byte-identical pin heroes **error**: a cross-post is two separate downloads and is never byte-identical (it surfaces perceptually), so byte-identical means one hero was written twice from one decode — the Thyssen bug wearing a link pin's clothes. **Places stay out of scope**, since a place hero may legitimately be a member's hero at the *same* URL (Legion of Honor, Hotel Casa del Mar, Oedo Antique Market). **Verification:** selftest **13/13**, **self-tested against 3 injected faults — 3/3 caught** (`--all` reverted to tours-only, the slug fallback removed, a pin duplicate downgraded to INFO); the perceptual pass confirmed to have teeth on the new data — **165 pairs nominated at Hamming ≤ 45, all rejected, closest 23.0 against a threshold of 8.0**. ⚠️ **`--pins` reports 240 images for 244 pins** — the five `@malata.antwerp` pins share one hero URL by design. **`--pins` is 10 seconds; `--all` is 7 minutes**, which is the whole reason six batches worked around this by hand. See `archive/HANDOFF-260830-2.md`. Earlier status follows.

Branch `claude/new-links-upload-qe46ns`, commit `5d4b24c5`; **no PR opened** (harness restriction), so **CI, the authoritative validator, has not run.** **🔴 All nine are ONE company on TWO channels** (`@WienerbergerAG`, `@WienerbergerOfficial`) and the maker id is uuid5 over `youtube:@handle`, so the catalogue now carries it as two creators — the id scheme working as designed, but the first time one organisation has appeared twice. **🔴 Every YouTube pin made before this has black bars baked into its hero:** oEmbed always returns `hqdefault.jpg`, 480×360, which for a 16:9 video is the frame **letterboxed** onto a 4:3 canvas, and the square crop kept the bars — which the app then shows, since it displays the middle square of the stored 1200×900. `best_thumbnail()` now prefers `maxresdefault.jpg` (same frame at 1280×720, nothing to trim, 2.7× the pixels) and `trim_bars()` strips uniform dark borders off whatever arrives. ⚠️ **`trim_bars` is deliberately conservative** — dark **AND** near-uniform, ≤30% off any edge, an implausible trim returns the original, pinned by a dark-*textured*-photo test — else it starts eating night photographs. ⚠️ **NOT backfilled: the 29 older pins keep their bars, and a correction means a NEW filename, never an overwrite (#567).** Also folds in the **handle-suffixed hero slug** #607 applied by hand (a bare subject slug is the stem the Atlas tour of the same subject already occupies), and adds **`--title`/`--focus`** for judgement the tool cannot infer: an awards channel titles every upload to a house pattern and composes thumbnails as a photograph **beside a text panel**, so a centred square slices the lettering mid-word. The source title survives verbatim in `longDescription`, and the id is uuid5 over `sourceURL` so neither flag can change it. Selftest **52 → 71**. **⚠️ Four heroes show the architect being interviewed rather than the building** — a pin re-hosts only the thumbnail and we never download the video, so no other frame exists; the fix, if wanted, is an owner-supplied photograph. **⚠️ Nine architects are absent from the vocabulary** (Christ & Gantenbein, Office Winhov, Sameep Padora, Tropical Space, Nicolás Campodonico, AleaOlea, BAAS Arquitectura), so all nine ship the generic `Designed by a Master`; adding them is a **code** change, kept out of a content batch. **⚠️ Two coordinates sit where the name does not and both are right** — Stadsarchief Delft is in `Den Hoorn` and Capilla San Bernardo ships `La Playosa`. **Verification:** 11 images to gh-pages by pure plumbing, tree diff **exactly 11 additions / 0 deletions / nothing outside `images/`**, none of the 11 among 5,704 existing image paths (`628d2399`, re-confirmed an ancestor of head after a parallel session cancelled the Pages deploy); **0** duplicate ids, **0** already-pinned sourceURLs, **0** orphans; a Python validator mirror (vocabulary parsed from **both** `Models/Tag.swift` and the Swift validator, refusing to run if they disagree or either parse is empty — **that guard fired on the first attempt**) self-tested **16/16** against injected faults, then clean at **0 errors, 0 warnings across 1512 tours + 38 pins**. See `archive/HANDOFF-260826-2.md`. Earlier status follows.

**Status (2026-08-25, session 110b):** web session (code) — **decode tolerance, stacked on the link-pin split.** Four fields had the fatal shape: `kind`, `primaryCategory`, `videoRole` and `triggerMode`. **🔴 Optional does not protect** — `decodeIfPresent` returns nil for an absent key or explicit null but **propagates the throw** for a key present with an unfamiliar value, so `videoRole` was exactly as fragile as the other three; pinned with a strict control enum. **Layer 1**: `triggerMode` → `.manual` (load-bearing — only `.geofenced` stops get a region, so an unknown rule can never auto-fire), `videoRole` → `.gallery` (b-roll, never seizes the transport), `primaryCategory` → `.culturalHeritage` (the widest bucket, claiming nothing the tour has not said). **🔴 `kind` deliberately does NOT fall back** — an unfamiliar pin shown as `.single` gets a play button with no audio, so it drops instead; there is a comment on the enum to stop someone "tidying" that into a default. **Layer 2, the real protection**: every array in `ToursData` decodes **element by element**, so one unreadable element costs that element and not the catalogue — covering every field nobody thought to guard. ⚠️ The obvious implementation (loop the unkeyed container, `try?` each element) can spin forever because `currentIndex` is not guaranteed to advance past a throw; wrapping each element in a type that cannot throw sidesteps it. ⚠️ **Drops are counted, not swallowed** — `CatalogDecodeLosses` plus one shared `decodeCatalog` helper that logs a non-zero total with its source named. ⚠️ **Two tests encoded the old contract and were changed deliberately with a comment explaining why**; `test_decodingTour_badKind_throws` is unchanged and still passes. **🔴 Tolerance protects only builds shipped after it — that is why the `linkPins` split comes first.** **Code PR — owner OK + a simulator look before merge.** See `archive/HANDOFF-260825-2.md`. Earlier status follows.

**Status (2026-08-25, session 110):** web session (code + content + backend) — **link pins move out of `tours`, and every frozen build starts receiving content again.** Four `kind: "link"` pins went live on 2026-08-24 at 22:51 and **froze every build before 116, silently**: `ToursData` decodes `tours` as one array, `TourKind` is a closed enum, so one unknown value fails the WHOLE catalogue, and `RemoteCatalogLoader`'s `try?` reads that as a failed fetch and keeps its last good copy without logging a thing. **🔴 An unknown top-level KEY is free; an unknown VALUE in a known field is fatal** — proven on this app's own history (`sourceURL`, `sourceAuthor`, `country`, `videoURLs`, `videoRole` and `places` all reached shipped builds unharmed; nothing broke until `kind: "link"` appeared *inside* a field builds already parsed). So pins now travel under a sibling **`linkPins`** array which the app merges back at decode. **🔴 This is the only change that reaches backwards — tolerance protects only builds shipped after it, and build 66 is at Apple in "Waiting for Review", strict forever.** Verified from its own source at `2bcf0df2`: `ToursData { makers, tours }`, `TourKind { single, multiStop }`, bundled catalogue **1,350 tours / 30 makers** against 1,516 live — **166 tours missing**, all of Barcelona, Milan and Stockholm. Proven against the real shipped `Resources/Tours.json`, not a fixture: build 66's transcribed decoder goes from **throwing at `.tours[1512].kind` → 0 tours** to **1,512 tours / 37 makers**, with `linkPins` and `places` both skipped as unknown keys. The same shape now holds in all four places — bundled seed, the gh-pages mirror (a byte-copy of it), `seed_from_toursjson.py` (folds pins back in; in Postgres a pin is still an ordinary `tours` row) and `get_catalog` via **`backend/split_link_pins.sql`**, which **patches the core and never touches `get_catalog()`**: it repeats the `places.sql` move, renaming `get_catalog_core` aside to `get_catalog_core_base` and wrapping it, so nothing is parsed and nothing can be dropped. ⚠️ **The new layer is deliberately SECURITY INVOKER** — `definer` would change who RLS evaluates the base as. `backend/test-migrations.sh` applies it against real Postgres: **3 migrations, idempotent, all keys and places intact, split asserted** — and goes red when the split is neutered on purpose. Three new guards close the hole that let this sit undetected: `check-catalog-keys.py`, the publish-catalog gate, and the validator all now refuse a link pin inside `tours`. ⚠️ **Accepted cost, owner-understood: builds 116–117 stop showing the pins until a build reads the new key.** **Code PR — owner OK + a simulator look before merge.** See `archive/HANDOFF-260825.md`. Earlier status follows.

**Status (2026-08-24, session 107, later):** the viewer is **merged** (squash `8d2ad947`) and shipped as **TestFlight 1.1 (114)**, cut from `main` so it carries [#569](https://github.com/ehky2882/TRAVEL-GUIDED-TOUR/pull/569), [#582](https://github.com/ehky2882/TRAVEL-GUIDED-TOUR/pull/582) and [#571](https://github.com/ehky2882/TRAVEL-GUIDED-TOUR/pull/571) together. `test_sim` **478/478**. **Status (2026-08-24, session 107):** code session (local Mac) — **the fullscreen video viewer** ([PR #571](https://github.com/ehky2882/TRAVEL-GUIDED-TOUR/pull/571), stacked on [#569](https://github.com/ehky2882/TRAVEL-GUIDED-TOUR/pull/569)). In the carousel a clip is letterboxed into the 320pt landscape hero box, so the vertical stand-in used **180pt of a 345pt box**; fullscreen it is 393×699, **4.8× the area**. **🔴 Three hit-testing traps, all with one symptom — a control that renders, sits in the accessibility tree, and whose action never runs.** A paged `TabView`'s `UIPageControl` spans the **full width** of its dot strip and swallowed the expand button's tap (the tell: the carousel advanced by exactly one page, every time); **two `.fullScreenCover` modifiers chained on one view silently drop the second**; and a 24pt inset put the close button inside the **Dynamic Island**, where the system takes the touch — while `GeometryReader` reports safe insets of **zero** once `.ignoresSafeArea()` is applied, so deriving the fix there moved the button not one pixel. **Presence in the accessibility tree says nothing about hit-testing — probe the action.** Rotation turns the **video, never the app** (`Info.plist` is a ceiling, and orientation belongs to the window scene the secondary window shares with the main one); vertical clips never rotate. ⚠️ **Clip shape must come from the display size after `preferredTransform`** — 1920×1080 with a 90° transform is a *vertical* clip. The narration debt is **transferred, not copied**, so the carousel cannot resume audio behind the video. `test_sim` **457/457**. ⚠️ **Rotation is device-only** and the catalogue still has **no landscape video**, so that half has never run against real content. Also cleared the `HANDOFF-260823-3.md` **name collision** — two different files — which was the only thing blocking #569. See `archive/HANDOFF-260824.md`. Earlier status follows.

**Status (2026-08-24, session 107):** web session (code) — **the chrome row stops being a slightly different colour than its page** ([PR #573](https://github.com/ehky2882/TRAVEL-GUIDED-TOUR/pull/573), squash `1232cbd`, **merged**). Owner marked two regions of a tour-detail screenshot: *"1 and 2 marked in the screencap are supposed to be same color."* They were. **The row drew `secondaryBackground.opacity(0.8)` over `.regularMaterial` on a page that is a plain `secondaryBackground` — and that token is a hardcoded RGB pair precisely so every painted surface resolves identically regardless of window or elevation**, which is the guarantee an 80%-over-a-material composite throws away. ⚠️ **The half nobody had named: a material samples what is behind it, so the row's shade DRIFTED as a hero photograph scrolled underneath** — a fixed offset would have been filed long ago, but a shade that only misbehaves mid-scroll just reads as vaguely off. **Status (2026-08-23, sessions 103b–104):** local Mac — **three fixes that each exposed a check which was not checking.** (1) **The coordinate fault is measured and proven upstream** ([PR #562](https://github.com/ehky2882/TRAVEL-GUIDED-TOUR/pull/562)): drop-pipeline cities run **41/50 north at a median +10.3 m, p = 5.6e-06**, while old-sourced New York and London sit at **24/51 and −0.9 m** — and that control is what makes the result believable rather than a tilted measuring stick. The gross errors are the same fault at a different scale: a constant **~20 px** map-click offset becomes a bigger ground distance the further you zoom out, which is why the worst errors are always the subjects furthest from the centre. Owner confirmed the coordinates are picked **by hand**; the fix is upstream and his to make. New `scripts/check-coordinates.py` (Automation Rule #8b) reports GROSS / UNVERIFIABLE / BIAS and **regression-catches both real Milan errors with no false positives**; the +10 m already shipped is deliberately left alone. Four of five were fixed free from the tour's own gallery. The tool now uses **curl not urllib** — urllib fails SSL on this Mac, which is how it printed *"OK"* having fetched nothing — **exits 2 with COULD NOT VERIFY** above a 20% failure rate, and **confirms candidates with a thumbnail comparison** after a bare average hash clustered the Empire State Building with the Pincio. **Status (2026-08-23, session 105b):** code session (local Mac) — **a downloaded tour brings its photographs** ([PR #567](https://github.com/ehky2882/TRAVEL-GUIDED-TOUR/pull/567)). From an owner report on the Underground. **The cause was not the splash:** gh-pages serves photos with `Cache-Control: max-age=600`, so although `App.init` sets a 200 MB `URLCache`, after ten minutes iOS revalidates with the server before using bytes it already holds — and with no server the photo is dropped. **🔴 Do NOT make the splash wait for "everything loaded"** — with no signal that never completes and the splash reads as frozen; the gate ceiling exists to prevent exactly that. Two owner asks, both watched in the Simulator rather than reasoned about. **The splash follows the colour scheme**: its ground was a hardcoded `.black`, and since `Info.plist` carries an empty `UILaunchScreen` dict — so iOS paints its own launch screen white in light mode — every light-mode launch ran **white → black → white**. The ground is `AtlasColors.background` now (which IS `systemBackground`, so dark mode is unchanged) and the wordmark `primaryText`; the brass disc and the hand-off choreography from #559 are untouched. **And the two light surface values swapped**: `background` is the ground and `secondaryBackground` the surface raised on it — dark had that already (`#1C1C1E` on `#000000`) while light had it backwards, so chrome sat in a haze a few percent off the page. Light is now chrome `#FFFFFF` over ground `#F2F2F7`; **dark is byte-identical**. Five palettes were built as a design canvas and the owner picked one. `test_sim` **418/418**; no SQL, no catalogue change, no build. ⚠️ Owed on device: the wizard fields, the sign-in sheet and the player, which draw the deep surface as a recessed field — the pairing that inverted. See `archive/HANDOFF-260822-3.md`. Earlier status follows.

**Status (2026-08-22, sessions 102–103):** code session — **the launch, end to end** ([PR #559](https://github.com/ehky2882/TRAVEL-GUIDED-TOUR/pull/559), squash `0954f585`, **merged**). Eleven TestFlight builds, **1.1 (99) → (109)**, owner-approved at 109: *"leave it here then. i like it."* `test_sim` **418/418**. **✅ The readiness gate** (build 99, owner-confirmed — *"definitely much more snappy and the animation is smooth"*): the splash waits for catalog + location + the first screenful of card photos, bounded by a 1.2s floor and a 3.0s ceiling, with `ContentView` mounted behind it, so `MKMapView`, the first clustering pass over 1,418 tours, the bars' window and the camera all resolve out of sight. **✅ The hand-off is three beats in 0.37s** — a solid brass disc expands from the user's own map dot and dissolves into a bare map; the bottom module arrives as one object with the drawer closed; the drawer opens as the search bar drops from the top and the capsules come in from the right, landing together on the frame the haptic fires. **🔴 The bug that cost most of those builds: `withAnimation` does not step a plain `Double` through its intermediate values** — SwiftUI jumps every view to the end state and animates each rendered property across the whole duration, so every delay and window in the choreography was evaluated at 1 and discarded. The progress is ticked at display rate now. Also fixed on the way: any body reading a 60Hz value redraws 60×/s (that was `ContentView` and `HomeView`), and `.offset` does not move a `.background`. Full detail, owner decisions and the rejected approaches: `archive/HANDOFF-260822.md`.

**Status (2026-08-21, session 101):** code + CI session (web) — **the upload wizard's second review round** ([PR #558](https://github.com/ehky2882/TRAVEL-GUIDED-TOUR/pull/558)). The owner walked the seven-step wizard on TestFlight **1.1 (96)** and sent notes step by step: eleven across six steps, plus two more as comments on a before/after review artifact. All eleven built; **TestFlight 1.1 (97)** then **(98)**, owner device-verified — *"I REVIEWED. GOOD."* No SQL, no catalogue change. **🔴 The headline is a layout modifier that never worked:** `maxHeight: .infinity` inside the wizard's step area does nothing, because a flexible child gets no room from a `ScrollView` whose content frame sets only a `minHeight` — so the comment describing "the step's one elastic element" described a mechanism that was not there. It produced three separate owner complaints (an undersized map on step 1, invisible boxes on step 2, a two-line transcript box on step 6) and was fixed three different ways: a real shape for the map, character-limit sizing for the fields, and a **measured** step area for the transcript box, which has no natural shape to give it. **⚠️ The more useful lesson is the overstatement that followed** — the PR flagged step 3 as broken too, the owner asked what that meant, and the answer was nothing: the symptom only appears where the stretched child has no size of its own, and five rows of tags have one. **Do not report the dead pattern as a defect without checking whether the child collapses.** Also this session: **Discard now reaches attached narration** (`removeAudio`, restoring the stop to `""`/`0` since both columns are `not null`) with **no confirmation on either branch**, matching the ✕ on a photo; the photo-reorder hint says **hold** rather than drag, since `.draggable` needs a half-second lift and the hold is what keeps a reorder apart from a scroll; **MAKE COVER** gives the common case a route needing no gesture; the transcript is a scrollable `TextEditor` with its controls pinned in **their own `safeAreaInset` above the footer**, because a step may not add to the one row that never changes between steps; and the Review step's preview map finally draws the pin, since `MakerTour` carries no stops and a page that prints a stop count shows that plainly. **🔴 And a build that succeeded and reported failure:** 1.1 (97) archived, signed, uploaded and processed, then went red because Apple rejected a `✕` in the build notes — `upload_to_testflight` writes the changelog *after* the upload, so the rejection landed seven minutes and one real build past the mistake, leaving a live installable build with no "What to Test". `scripts/ascii-build-notes.py` now transliterates first. **⚠️ Open:** the no-scroll rule now holds on a 6.3″ phone and **not on an SE** (step 2 overflows ~94pt); Dynamic Type is still assumed off; the owner has further comments, to be taken on a fresh branch. Earlier status follows.

**Previously (2026-08-19, session 99):** code + content + SQL session (web) — **the Settings screen stops lying about itself** ([PR #544](https://github.com/ehky2882/TRAVEL-GUIDED-TOUR/pull/544), squash `4604bef`, **merged**; TestFlight **1.1 (85) → (86)**, owner device-verified). Four fixes, two of which were bugs rather than the polish that was asked for. **The wordmark** now matches `SplashView` exactly (Title Case, 15pt serif, tracked 2) — and is **actually brass**, which it never was: `accent`/`mapPin` are `Color.accentColor`, resolved from the environment, and this screen deliberately pins `.tint(primaryText)` to stop the system auto-tinting every row icon gold, which repainted the wordmark white for months while its call site plainly said `mapPin`. New `AtlasColors.brass` holds the value as a literal, immune to tint. **The version** read a hardcoded `Version 1.0` while the app target has been on 1.1 since build 46, so every tester was told the wrong version; it now reads the bundle and renders `Version 1.1 (86)`. **Makers → Dozents**, counting all 39 accounts including the 7 that have published nothing (owner decision). **Cities and Countries** added, counted from the catalog — `country` did not exist anywhere before and went into the **catalog data** rather than a Swift lookup, because a city launch merges as content and reaches phones with no build; all 103 cities were mapped explicitly then cross-checked against the bureau that authored each. One owner SQL paste (`backend/add_country.sql`) applied and verified live. Catalog size unchanged at **1,418 tours / 31 makers / 1,774 stops**; five NYC tours had `city` corrected from "New York City" to "New York". See `archive/HANDOFF-260819-6.md`.

**Previously (2026-08-19, session 99):** code session (web) — **the tour upload is a five-step wizard, and the three screens it replaces are deleted** ([PR #540](https://github.com/ehky2882/TRAVEL-GUIDED-TOUR/pull/540)). Owner supplied a design handoff and asked for a mockup before any code; the flow is **Location → Details → Photos → Audio → Review**, with a tappable progress bar as the index and a Back / Save progress / Next footer on every step. `CreateTourView`, `TourAuthoringView`, `TourDetailsEditorView` and `PhotoManagerView` are **gone** (~1,400 lines) — the wizard is now the only way a maker creates *or* edits, at the owner's direction ("i only want the wizard"). Photos are a filled-slot grid to **7** whose empty slots are themselves pickers; framing and recording both happen **inline on their step** rather than on pages of their own, and the recorder gained a live level meter. **Nothing but a title is required** — owner decision that corrected a gate I had invented, and it matches `validate-tours.swift`, which treats a missing tag as a warning and never an error. Gating lives in pure, unit-tested `TourWizardRules` where `canAdvance` is defined as `blockingReason(...) == nil`, so a dimmed button always has a stated reason. **🔴 It shipped with a hang that took three builds to find, and the durable lesson is about `.interactiveDismissDisabled`:** opening a saved tour wedged the main thread until the watchdog killed the app, because that modifier read the live map coordinate — and since `CLLocationCoordinate2D` is not `Equatable`, every camera callback dirtied the **root** body and reconfigured `UISheetPresentationController`, which laid the map out again. The crash log said so in one frame (`NavigationStack.init(root:)` inside `_sheetLayoutInfoLayout:`); two builds were spent removing plausible suspects before reading it properly. **The old `CreateTourView` wrote the coordinate identically for a year without hanging** — it simply had no dismiss guard, which is what diffing the deleted screen against the new one revealed. Fixed by gating coordinate writes on a tolerance, feeding the guard from plain `Bool` state computed below the root, and stopping a programmatic field fill from firing the typed-in-it path. **The hang outlived six diagnoses (builds 84→89) and was finally caused by one line of my own code: `centerOnUser` skipped the edit path, leaving `Map(position:)` on `.automatic` with no pin, circle or annotation to frame — an automatic camera over empty content resolves, writes back through the binding, re-renders and resolves again, forever. It is the only theory that ever explained why CREATING a tour never hung, and it was found by diffing the two paths rather than by reading another crash stack. Build 90 starts the camera at a concrete region.** ⚠️ **The durable lesson: a crash log is one sample of a spin** — its top frame named a different function in every log (`NavigationStack` body, `ToolbarBridge.updateLocations()`, the Map's trait walk) and each produced a plausible, wrong fix. **TestFlight 1.1 (90) is owner device-verified — the hang is closed.** ⚠️ Two follow-ups remain: the edit path is still `.fullScreenCover` (shipped as a fix for a cause that proved wrong — reverting to `.sheet` is reasonable as its own change), and PR #540 is open awaiting the owner's merge call. Earlier status follows.

**Status (2026-08-18, session 95):** code + content + SQL session (web) — **the place layer: a site described by several tours now has its own pin, card and page, and that page can be saved and shared.** Step 1 (data layer) squash `82c3b33`; **[PR #523](https://github.com/ehky2882/TRAVEL-GUIDED-TOUR/pull/523)** carries the map/page work and the polish pass. **24 places, all in `Tours.json`; catalog untouched at 1350 tours / 30 makers / 1696 stops.** The durable answer to session 93's coincident-pin bug, which stacked place cards as a stopgap: the site becomes the thing on the map and the tours become its contents. **Identity is exact coordinate equality** (owner decision) — a 40 m proximity rule was measured against the live catalog first and rejected, producing **43 places of which 19 were wrong** (it merged LACMA with the Academy Museum and chained three La Boca venues transitively); the validator enforces the exact rule. **Ranking is newest-first, explicitly provisional** — the design brief assumed listen counts, which do not exist anywhere in the codebase, so the page *states* the order rather than offering a sort control it cannot honour; both tiebreaks in `Place.ranked` are load-bearing because 22 of the 24 places have tours published in the same batch with identical `createdAt`. **🐛 The build that cost the most: I told the owner "nothing for you to run yet."** Build 68 shipped the code and showed the old stacked cards, because **the app reads Supabase first** and `places.sql` had never been applied — the RPC served no `places` key, so the new pin could not exist. One dashboard paste fixed it with no new build. **Adding a key to `Tours.json` does not put it in front of users.** The page was then **rebuilt on `TourDetailView`'s structure** at the owner's direction, settled from an HTML mockup of all three screens side by side at true 390 pt proportion — nine divergences found by reading the two files against each other, all three open decisions taken by the owner (Gallery/Map strip **yes**, brass `N TOURS AVAILABLE` header **kept**, place carousel **yes**). Saving is a new `SavedPlacesStore` (UserDefaults, works signed out) rather than a second concern hung off `LibraryStore`, whose `onChange` hook means *push the tour library to Supabase*; a place bookmark is a plain toggle because a place has no lists a second tap could destroy. Sharing adds `DeepLink.place`, the `p` marker, `AtlasShareLink.placeURL` and a `p/index.html` landing page on gh-pages (`ee5c1fb`) so a shared link degrades to a real page rather than a 404. **Step 4 landed in the same PR:** all 24 places carry editorial copy and an address, and 20 carry a photograph of their own. ⚠️ **13 of the 24 had been showing one photograph three times** — both tours at such a place carry the same hero file, because a walk's intro stop reuses the single tour's photograph, and the place borrowed it again; fixed for 12 by promoting images already uploaded and already verified, every candidate **opened** and five first picks rejected as close-ups. **Square Saint-Louis is the one place still repeating an image** and needs a single sourced photo. ⚠️ **Addresses are editorial, corroborated by reverse geocoding rather than taken from it** — Nominatim returned a neighbouring shop for two places, a side street for a third and a viewpoint for a fourth; `GET DIRECTIONS` routes on coordinates, so a soft address cannot misdirect anyone. **`Also at <place>` on tour detail** surfaces the sibling tours from inside a tour, where most people arrive, rather than only from the map. **Saved places now sync** on the same terms as saved tours; ⚠️ the merge keeps the **earlier** `savedAt`, the opposite of recently-viewed, because the Library list is ordered by it. **🐛 `revoke-dev-certs.py` had been running before the signing key existed since the July fastlane rewrite** — `key_path` is lazy and was reached three steps later, so the script found no key, exited, and freed no certificate slots; build 67 died at the cap. Fixed in both lanes. **Builds 69 → 73 then chased four device bugs, and the durable one is architectural: a presenter must tear down its own layer.** `dismiss()` only nilled the presented value and relied on an `.onChange` in `ContentView` — the **main** window, which a layer covers completely — to collapse the UIKit layer; SwiftUI can stop delivering updates to a covered hierarchy, so on *tour → maker → close maker → tap a tab* the state cleared while the layer stayed up and the tab bar read as dead. Two earlier attempts missed it: [#526](https://github.com/ehky2882/TRAVEL-GUIDED-TOUR/pull/526) (a genuinely missing `PlacePresenter` injection) and [#528](https://github.com/ehky2882/TRAVEL-GUIDED-TOUR/pull/528), which exempted places from clustering on a **wrong theory** that a clustered place pin was unreachable. Fixed in [#535](https://github.com/ehky2882/TRAVEL-GUIDED-TOUR/pull/535) with a `performDismiss` closure on every presenter. #528 was then reverted by [#536](https://github.com/ehky2882/TRAVEL-GUIDED-TOUR/pull/536), which also made a cluster badge count **tours** rather than markers — the exemption had made the map lie, floating a lone `2` capsule beside a `100` cluster at world zoom. **Both wrong theories were reasoned from code; both were settled in one image by an owner screenshot.** **1.1 (73) carries both and is owner device-verified** — the stuck-tour case and the world-zoom clustering are closed. **Two owner screenshots on 2026-08-19 closed the last of it, and both fixes are in 1.1 (78), owner-verified.** 🔴 **A slide-up layer must switch the bottom bars to edge-to-edge:** the island form's 8 pt gaps exist so the map shows through on Home, so a place page that left the island form showed *its own* tour rows under the tab bar. All three terms of `extendsToScreenEdges` were false for a place opened from Home — `PlaceView` never calls `navState.push()` — and the reserved height was never the problem, since it is constant across both forms. **Two lists had to name every layer** and the place layer shipped missing from one in 1.1 (69) and the other in 1.1 (73), so they are now the single property `isAnyLayerPresented` ([#543](https://github.com/ehky2882/TRAVEL-GUIDED-TOUR/pull/543)). **AMNH became a place by fixing a coordinate, not a rule:** its two pins were 107 m apart because the single tour's pin sat mid-block while its script is entirely about the Central Park West entrance; corrected, the pair is coincident and groups under the existing exact rule ([#541](https://github.com/ehky2882/TRAVEL-GUIDED-TOUR/pull/541), places 24 → 25). A catalog-wide survey found **398 un-grouped pairs within 200 m** — LACMA and the Academy Museum are 22 m apart, which is why distance was rejected — but only **two** where one title is a prefix of the other, the second being Tibidabo / Tibidabo Amusement Park, deliberately left separate. See `archive/HANDOFF-260818.md` and `archive/HANDOFF-260819-3.md`. Earlier status follows.

**Status (2026-08-17, session 93):** code session (web) — **fixed map pins that swallowed every tap, on both the home map and the creator page, plus a drawer/rail disagreement underneath.** [PR #512](https://github.com/ehky2882/TRAVEL-GUIDED-TOUR/pull/512) (squash `6824b26`); **TestFlight 1.1 (64), owner device-verified good.** Catalog untouched at **1350 tours / 30 makers / 1696 stops**. Reported from a Montreal screenshot: a "2" cluster pin over Dorchester Square that no tap or zoom would open. **Three defects.** (1) **Two different tours sit at exactly `45.4997, -73.5710`** — the single-stop *Dorchester Square and the Sun Life Building* and the intro of the *Downtown and the Underground City* walk, which correctly begins at that square. Clustering buckets by grid cell, so identical points share a cell at **every** cell pitch: no camera can separate them and the tap was an infinite no-op. **24 such pairs catalog-wide**, every one a walk intro on the single-stop tour of the same landmark (Dam Square, Rijksmuseum, Westerkerk, Colosseum, Brandenburg Gate, CN Tower, ROM, Walt Disney Concert Hall…), hitting **7 of the 30 maker pages** as well as the home map. Both maps now raise **one placecard per tour, stacked above the pin**. (2) **A cluster tap could zoom OUT** — the framing helper floored its span at 0.01° (~1.1 km) while clusters routinely form far below that, so framing one *widened* the camera and re-rendered the same pin; it now clamps to half the live span. (3) **The drawer header and the In-view rail used different definitions of "in view"** — any-stop vs the tour's centroid — so a walk whose first stop was on screen but whose centroid sat 197 m away was counted in the header and missing from the list; the rail now matches the header. **Owner picked the maker-map treatment from an interactive mockup of three options** ("i actually prefer c"), choosing stacked cards over the recommended action sheet for consistency; fitting them into a 320pt map needed a new `MapClustering.region(anchoring:at:span:)` that sits the pin 72% down the frame. `needsDisambiguation` + `PlacecardView.standardWidth` are now shared by both maps so they can't drift. **⚠️ This bug class is invisible to every check we run** — the validator passes, CI compiles, the URLs all resolve; only tapping the pin reveals it. **⚠️ It is structural and will recur with every new city that ships a walk starting at a landmark that also has its own tour.** New `MapClusteringSeparationTests` pins the invariants.

Worth fixing the script to fail loudly. **Verification: 0 errors / 0 warnings across all 1466 tours via `swift scripts/validate-tours.swift` itself** — a Mac session, so the authoritative validator rather than a Python mirror; uuid5 reverse-verified 8/8 before minting MIL; 0 duplicate ids across 1466/1828/32; 0 slug collisions against the live catalog and all 6,887 gh-pages paths; **182 uploaded = 182 referenced, 0 orphaned**; gh-pages via pure plumbing (exactly **236 additions, 0 deletions**, nothing outside `audio/`+`images/`, 0 of 236 paths pre-existing, commit `2a718e9`), then all 236 live URLs hash-verified against the uploaded blobs; Tours.json byte-stable before editing, diff **2,302 insertions / 0 deletions**. ⚠️ `Abbazia di Mirasole` ships `city: "Opera"` (different comune, the Montserrat convention); 4 tours ship hero-only; all images owner-supplied so no CREDITS rows. ⚠️ **Nine Milan architects are absent from the tag vocabulary** and ship `Designed by a Master` — Muzio, BBPR/Rogers, Boeri, Chipperfield, Mengoni, Bramante, Maciachini, Beltrami, Cucinella — strengthening the standing `Models/Tag.swift` PR case; `Rem Koolhaas` was already present and is used on Fondazione Prada. it emitted 4,687 bogus errors, which is the only reason it was noticed — a subtler parse failure would have passed everything silently. **Assert a non-empty parse, don't just run the mirror.** Final **0 errors / 0 warnings across all 1040 tours**, self-tested against **18 injected fault classes (18/18 caught)**; **Status (2026-07-25, session 70):** CI/docs session — **every TestFlight build now carries its own description inside TestFlight, in the build's "What to Test" field.** Owner ask: *"i need to know what new features are added when we cut new builds."* **Owner-confirmed working** on a build cut from `main`. It took **three PRs and three blank builds** to get there: [#425](https://github.com/ehky2882/TRAVEL-GUIDED-TOUR/pull/425) (`4c20bd3`) added the mechanism using fastlane `set_changelog` — which **cannot target a TestFlight build** (it sets the App Store version's release notes and rejects `build_number`), so build 1.1 (36) shipped blank; [#430](https://github.com/ehky2882/TRAVEL-GUIDED-TOUR/pull/430) (`e1ff0fe`) switched to **`upload_to_testflight` with `distribute_only: true`**, which then *prompted* for the app platform and crashed in CI (*"Could not retrieve response as fastlane runs in non-interactive mode"*), costing builds (39) + (40); [#437](https://github.com/ehky2882/TRAVEL-GUIDED-TOUR/pull/437) (`432241d`) added **`app_platform: "ios"`** — mandatory in CI despite the docs calling it optional — and it worked. **The durable lesson is the retry classifier, not fastlane:** both failures stayed invisible for 20–25 min because the loop treated every error as "Apple is still processing," the second time via a loose grep on the bare word `processing` that matched fastlane's own option list (`skip_waiting_for_build_processing`). It now checks permanent signatures first, retries only on precise phrases, and **defaults to permanent** — a wrong "permanent" call costs nothing, a wrong "retryable" call hides the error. Also codified: **`workflow_dispatch` uses the workflow file from the branch you build FROM**, so a `testflight.yml` fix doesn't reach a feature-branch build until that branch merges `main`. A green run does **not** imply notes attached — read the **Done** line. Notes fall back input → PR title+body → commit subject (4000-char cap); job timeout 40 → 70 min. Branch-delete owed via GitHub UI. See `archive/HANDOFF-260725.md`. Earlier status follows.

**Status (2026-06-18, session 41):** infra session — **catalog publishing is now automated; the manual gh-pages re-upload step is gone.** New [`.github/workflows/publish-catalog.yml`](.github/workflows/publish-catalog.yml) ([PR #212](https://github.com/ehky2882/TRAVEL-GUIDED-TOUR/pull/212), squash-merged, CI green) closes the drift gap PR #209 left: it triggers on every push to `main` that touches `TRAVEL GUIDED TOUR/Resources/Tours.json` (path-filtered) plus manual `workflow_dispatch`, validates the file (JSON parse + non-empty `tours`/`makers`), and copies **only** `Tours.json` to the gh-pages root — committing only on a real diff (`[skip ci]` message) and pushing via the built-in `GITHUB_TOKEN`. Audio/images/privacy on gh-pages are never touched; it pushes only to gh-pages so it can't retrigger itself. **What this means for the owner:** merge a `Tours.json` change to `main` → it auto-publishes to gh-pages within ~1 min → the live app picks it up on next launch. No manual upload, no app rebuild; the 272-vs-300 drift can't recur. gh-pages `Tours.json` is now workflow-owned — never hand-edited. Real-run verified via `workflow_dispatch` (SUCCESS; correctly no-op'd since already in sync). Earlier status follows.

**Status (2026-06-18, session 40):** verify-task-turned-ship — **the catalog detached from the app bundle ([PR #209](https://github.com/ehky2882/TRAVEL-GUIDED-TOUR/pull/209)) and TestFlight 1.0 (46) cut and shipped.** PR #209 makes the app fetch `Tours.json` from gh-pages at launch — it loads the local copy first (last-good network cache → bundled seed) for an instant, offline-capable first frame, then refreshes from the URL in the background and republishes live; any network/decode failure keeps the local copy intact. **So content can now ship by pushing one file to gh-pages — no app rebuild, no App Store review** (the first backend seam). New `Data/RemoteCatalogLoader.swift` (fetch behind a `CatalogFetching` protocol) + injectable loader/`autoRefresh` on `DataService`; bundled `Tours.json` kept as the offline seed; **95/95 tests** (7 new `RemoteCatalogLoaderTests` cover cache/bundle/nil load + fetch-error + undecodable fallbacks). Fixed: **republished gh-pages `Tours.json` to 300** (verified live), proved the live update end-to-end (added a ★ to one title on gh-pages → relaunch showed it with no rebuild → reverted clean), and **caught the PR branch up to 300** before merging so the offline seed ships 300 too. **Status (2026-06-06, session 24):** every V1 functionality milestone is shipped on `main`. Implementation session — **full-screen Player polish, round 2**, owner-driven at the simulator (continues session 22). The structural change: `PlayerView` is now presented as a `.fullScreenCover` from `BottomModuleRoot` (the top window that hosts the mini-player + tab bar) rather than from `ContentView`, so the cover slides over the module *in the same window* — eliminating the transition gap that the old hide/show-the-module-window approach left behind (removed `setHidden` entirely; `PassThroughWindow.hitTest` claims all touches while presenting a modal). Opening the player also dismisses the underlying detail sheet, so retracting returns to the tab root and Home shows its floating island (not edge-to-edge). Plus: single-line/marquee stop title, caption that always reserves 3 lines, and the system volume slider bracketed by speaker icons with a smaller thumb. **No build bump (33). No `AudioPlayerService` API change. 88/88 tests pass.** Drag-to-dismiss + volume/AirPlay still need a real-device check. Earlier status follows.

**Status (2026-06-06, session 23):** implementation session — **place search added to Search**. Typing a place name (e.g. "London", "Brooklyn") now surfaces a **Places** section above the Makers/Tours catalog results; tapping a place dismisses Search and glides the Home map camera to that region. Backed by Apple's `MKLocalSearch` (no new dependencies, no backend — same MapKit/CoreLocation already in the app). New `PlaceSearchService` (debounced 300ms, cancels stale lookups, derives per-feature zoom from the placemark radius); `SearchView` gains the Places section + a one-shot `HomeSharedState.pendingMapMove` channel to the map; `HomeView` flies the camera (additive — recenter / pin-tap / startup paths untouched) and shows a transient "No Atlas tours here yet" hint when the destination has no tour pins (decision: go-anywhere + overlay). New `MapRegionGeometry.anyStop(of:inside:)` reuses the existing antimeridian-aware `MKCoordinateRegion.contains`. **88/88 tests pass (4 new).** This explicitly lifted the prior "Home map camera is settled — don't touch" constraint, per owner approval, for this additive change. Also folds in the same day's Search-polish commit (caption typography, single-line result rows, maker result section). Known: the no-tours hint can paint a few seconds late **in the simulator** (MKMapView tile-streaming starves SwiftUI overlay compositing) — verify prompt on device; one cosmetic `placemark` iOS-26 deprecation warning left as a follow-up. No build bump (stays 33). TestFlight unchanged.

**Content (M-launch-content ✅).** **97 tours** in `Resources/Tours.json` (73 NYC + 17 Porto-area + 2 Marco de Canaveses/Lisbon/Cascais/Matosinhos under Atlas Studio Lisbon and Atlas Studio Porto).
First **multi-stop tour** added 2026-05-26: "American Museum of Natural
History: Four Facades" — 5 stops (~8m 44s), exterior architecture walk,
geofenced. This unblocks M-qa checklist items 6 + 7. The original 10 NYC landmarks (Grand Central, Times
Square, South Street Seaport, Empire State Building, Statue of Liberty,
**Home polish + player-state hardening (PR #60, 2026-05-24 late-pm).**
Bigger bottom-module radius (48→56), drawer now overlays the mini-player +
tab bar (squared bottom corners via new `bottomReservedHeight` /
`bottomCornerRadius: 0`), chip row + search bar share `searchBarHeight =
46`, `caption` font throughout, "tours in view" count math fixed (uses
individual stops + screen-coord math; swaps to "Let's explore together!"
at the `.large` detent), recenter button tracks the drawer's top edge in
every detent. Same PR fixed three audio-state bugs surfaced during the
visual review: (1) `TourDetailView` no longer disables "Open player"
mid-load — the sheet is the user's escape hatch; (2)
`AudioPlayerService.seek(to:)` synthesizes `.ended` when the user scrubs
to/past `duration` (AVPlayer doesn't fire `didPlayToEndTime` on manual
seek, so without this we'd park in `.waitingToPlayAtSpecifiedRate` →
flip to `.loading`); (3) `PlayerView.togglePlayPause` on `.ended` now
calls a new `replayCurrent()` that restarts via `playIntro`/`playStop`
(was `audioPlayer.play()`, a no-op on the drained AVQueuePlayer queue).
Commit `e5b31da`. 84/84 tests pass; CI green.

**Module geometry on non-Home tabs (PR #66, 2026-05-25).** Extends PR
#60's bottom-module work past the home screen. On Home the mini-player +
tab bar still float as a rounded island; on Library / Settings / Manage
downloads / Tour Detail / Maker the module now extends flush to the
screen edges (no horizontal inset, no rounded outer corners) and its
background runs through the home-indicator safe area, with the button
column padded up so taps still clear the indicator. Every scrollable
non-Home surface now applies `.safeAreaInset(.bottom)` sized to the
module so the last list item / settings row / tour-detail description is
always reachable above the module. Mechanism: new
`AtlasBottomModule.height(extendsToScreenEdges:)` helper centralizes the
math (replaces HomeView's inlined `floatingIslandHeight = 64 +
layoutHeight`); `MiniPlayerBar` and `AtlasTabBar` each gain an
`extendsToScreenEdges` flag. TourDetailView's `actionBarHeight` now
tracks the helper (was hardcoded `130 + layoutHeight + 8`) and its
trailing ScrollView spacer matches `actionBarHeight` exactly — fixes the
pre-existing too-small 72pt spacer that let the last description lines
hide behind the action bar. Commit `2452f52`. 84/84 tests pass; CI green.

**Restore Home floating island + anchor at OLD Home position (PR #69,
2026-05-25 pm-2).** PR #68 introduced two compounding regressions on
Home that the owner caught immediately. (1) `AtlasTabBar` added the
home-indicator safe-area inset to the view's *height* in full-edge
mode — physically shoved the buttons + mini-player up by ~34pt. (2)
The PreferenceKey-driven `moduleGeometry` got stuck at `.fullEdge`
after popping back from a detail screen, leaving Home in the wrong
geometry. Net effect: Home no longer floated AND everything sat too
high. Fix: `AtlasTabBar.body` is now a fixed-height VStack (56pt
painted button row + 8pt outer strip) in both modes; only what's
painted in the 8pt strip changes (transparent on Home, opaque
elsewhere). The safe-area zone underneath is already covered by the
painted button row because of the parent `.ignoresSafeArea(.bottom)`.
`AtlasBottomModule.height` is a constant 126pt across modes.
Replaced PreferenceKey with `@Observable AtlasNavigationState`
tracking `pushedDepth` via push/pop from each pushed view's
onAppear/onDisappear — deterministic, no sticky values. Verified via
`snapshot_ui`: tab buttons at y=807 in every tab (Home, Library, Me),
matching the OLD Home position exactly. Commit `8d928b3`. 84/84
tests pass; CI green.

**Buttons identical across surfaces; only background fill differs (PR
#70, 2026-05-25 pm — final shape).** Bar contents render the exact same
form on every surface — Home, Library, Me, every pushed detail. 8pt
horizontal inset, phone-screen-radius rounded bottom corners,
transparent 8pt strip below. The only difference between Home
(floating island) and the rest (full-edge): `ContentView` paints an
edge-to-edge `secondaryBackground` rectangle BEHIND the inset bar on
non-Home surfaces, making the side + bottom gaps blend into a
continuous strip the same color as the bar. On Home that fill isn't
painted, so the gaps show the map. `AtlasTabBar` + `MiniPlayerBar`
lost their `extendsToScreenEdges` flags entirely (single form now).
Final snapshot_ui anchor: tab buttons at x=8 / 136.67 / 265.33 width
128.67 on every surface, identical to OLD Home. Commit `643cbd7`.
84/84 tests pass; CI green.

**Restore Home floating island after PR #68 regression (PR #69,
2026-05-25 pm).** PR #68 made non-Home's tab bar 34pt taller (by
adding the safe-area inset to its painted height), which physically
shoved buttons up — opposite of the "anchor at OLD Home position"
goal. Its PreferenceKey-driven `moduleGeometry` also got stuck at
`.fullEdge` after popping back from a detail, so Home rendered in
the wrong (full-edge) shape with the buttons in the wrong (higher)
position. PR #69 fixed both: `AtlasTabBar` view height pinned at a
fixed 64pt in every geometry (56pt painted button row + 8pt outer
strip — only the strip's fill differs between modes); replaced the
preference with an `@Observable AtlasNavigationState` tracking
`pushedDepth` via push/pop from each pushed view's
appear/disappear; `AtlasBottomModule.height` locked to a constant
126pt. Commit `8d928b3`. 84/84 tests pass; CI green. Superseded
mechanism-wise by PR #70 (which collapsed even the remaining
conditional bottom-strip fill into a single fill-rectangle
decision in `ContentView`).

**Consistent bottom module across tabs + detail (PR #68, 2026-05-25 pm).**
Visual review of PRs #65 + #66 on `main` caught three follow-ups that
PR #66 left on the floor. (1) `SearchBar` was presenting `SearchView` as
`.sheet(...)`, which covered `ContentView`'s ZStack — the mini-player +
tab bar disappeared when the user opened search, and the further
`TourDetailView` push inherited the sheet's own nav stack. Switched to a
`NavigationLink` push into the host tab's stack so the module stays
visible underneath; `SearchView` dropped its inner `NavigationStack` +
"Close" toolbar and now applies a `safeAreaInset(.bottom)` so result
rows clear the module. (2) `AtlasTabBar` was adding the home-indicator
safe-area inset *inside* the painted button row in full-edge mode,
shoving the buttons ~26pt higher than they sat on Home — the bar
appeared to jump up when switching tabs. Refactored so the safe-area
fill is a separate background rectangle below an identically-laid-out
button row; only what's painted below changes between modes. (3) Detail
screens (`TourDetailView` / `MakerView` / `ManageDownloadsView` /
`SearchView`) used to inherit the Home tab's floating-island look when
pushed from Home, which let scrolled content peek through the 8pt outer
transparent gap below the rounded tab bar. Now every detail screen
declares the full-edge geometry directly and overrides its host tab's
preference while it's on top. Mechanism: replaced `\.atlasIsHomeTab` env
value (deleted) with a typed `AtlasModuleGeometry` preference; each
surface declares its preference at its root; the deepest declaration
wins; `ContentView` reads via `onPreferenceChange` and threads geometry
into `MiniPlayerBar` + `AtlasTabBar`. `AtlasBottomModule.height` math
updated to add the new 8pt outer gap above the safe-area fill on
non-Home. Commit `fe11d99`. 84/84 tests pass; CI green.

## V1 — Functionality milestones (in execution order)

### M1–M3. Build infrastructure — ✅ Done

These three milestones built the structural shell that survives the
audio-tour pivot. Brief status:

| | Status |

### M-data-model. New data model — Tour, Stop, Maker, LibraryEntry, TourCategory, RecentSearch — ✅ Done (PR #6)

**What:** Add the new Swift model types described in
`atlas_claude_code_prompt.md` § Data Model. Add a one- or two-tour
`Tours.json` for shape testing. Reshape `DataService` to load it.
Delete the old `City` / `Place` / `PlaceCategory` /
`PlaceCollection` models.

**Why:** Every later milestone depends on this shape. The previous
data model doesn't fit the audio-tour product at all.

**Files touched:**
- `Models/Tour.swift`, `Models/Stop.swift`, `Models/Maker.swift`,
  `Models/LibraryEntry.swift` (new)
- `Models/TourCategory.swift` (new — closed enum of categories that
  drive the home screen's interest-based rails; spec § TourCategory)
- `Models/RecentSearch.swift` (new — local-only record of a search
  query, used by the "Because you searched [X]" rail; spec §
  RecentSearch)
- `Resources/Tours.json` (new — shape-test content only; real content
  arrives in M-launch-content)
- `Data/DataService.swift` (reshape)
- `Data/SeedData.swift` (rename → `Data/ToursData.swift` or rewrite
  in place)
- `Resources/SeedData.json` (delete once `Tours.json` works)
- `Models/{City,Place,PlaceCategory,PlaceCollection}.swift` (delete)

**Expected fallout:** Most existing views (`DiscoverView`,
`CityDetailView`, `PlaceDetailView`, `MapView`, `CollectionsView`)
break at compile time after the model swap. That's expected — the
next milestones rewrite them. To keep the app buildable mid-milestone,
the views can be stubbed to "Coming soon" placeholders until their
own milestone lands.

**How we know it worked:** App compiles; `DataService` exposes
`tours: [Tour]` on the environment; a debug print confirms tours
loaded.

### M-audio-foundation. Audio playback infrastructure — ✅ Done (PR #7)

**What:** Wire up audio playback as a foundation every later milestone
will use.

- Add `Audio/AudioPlayerService.swift` (`@Observable`, on the
  environment shelf) wrapping `AVQueuePlayer`.
- Configure `AVAudioSession` with `.playback` category (audio
  continues with phone locked, ducks other audio appropriately).
- Add `UIBackgroundModes` → `audio` to Info.plist build settings.
- Wire `MPNowPlayingInfoCenter` and `MPRemoteCommandCenter` for play /
  pause / scrub / skip-forward / skip-backward from lock screen,
  headphones, AirPods, and CarPlay.

**Why:** Audio playback is the product's core feature; everything
downstream is wallpaper around it. Best to get the iOS plumbing right
once.

**Files touched:**
- `Audio/AudioPlayerService.swift` (new)
- `project.pbxproj` (add `UIBackgroundModes` build setting)
- `TRAVEL_GUIDED_TOURApp.swift` (instantiate service, place on
  environment)

**How we know it worked:** A throwaway test view plays a known remote
audio URL → audio plays → lock phone → audio continues → unlock →
lock-screen controls show the playing audio with title/artwork → tap
pause from lock screen → audio pauses.

### M-home. Map-dominant home screen with curated rails — ✅ Done (PR #10; redesigned in PR #19)

> PR #19's full-screen-map redesign replaced the stacked rails with
> a single distance-sorted tour list in a bottom drawer. The shipping
> home has no rails — `HomeRailsViewModel` and `RailCarousel` are
> unused by the app (still exercised by the unit suite). The old
> "Because you searched [X]" rail is retired along with the layout.

**What:** Build the new home screen, modeled on the Airbnb landing
page pattern. See `atlas_claude_code_prompt.md` § Key screens #1 for
the design contract. Major pieces:

1. **Search bar pinned to the top** above the map (taps to open the
   M-search results screen).
2. **Map** filling the upper portion of the screen, with tour-stop
   pins. Centered on the user's location, or a sensible default if
   denied / unavailable. Panning the map updates the location-anchored
   rails below.
3. **Curated horizontal-scroll rails** filling the lower portion.
   Three rail families:
   - **Location-anchored:** "Near you" (uses `LocationManager`),
     and "In [city/area in view]" that recomputes when the map pans.
   - **Personalized:** "Continue listening" (`LibraryEntry.listenedSeconds > 0
     && completedAt == nil`), "Recently viewed" (small local cache),
     "Because you searched [X]" (from `RecentSearch`).
   - **Interest-based:** one rail per `TourCategory` — "History,"
     "Architecture," "Art," etc. Hide rails with zero matching tours.

Also: tweak the location-permission copy in build settings to match
the audio-tour framing.

**Files touched:**
- `Features/Home/HomeView.swift` (new)
- `Features/Home/HomeMapSection.swift` (new — map at top, with pan
  detection that updates rail state)
- `Features/Home/RailCarousel.swift` (new — reusable horizontal-
  scroll rail component used by every rail family)
- `Features/Home/HomeRailsViewModel.swift` (new — computes which
  rails to surface, sorts tours within each rail)
- `Features/Discover/` (delete after move)
- `ContentView.swift` (point Home tab at the new view)
- `project.pbxproj` (update `NSLocationWhenInUseUsageDescription` copy)

**How we know it worked:** Set simulated location to NYC → map opens
on NYC with pins → "Near you" rail shows NYC tours → category rails
show interest-organized tours. Pan map to Lisbon → location-anchored
rails recompute. Deny location → map opens on a sensible default
view, location-anchored rails fall back to "Browse all" or hide.

### M-search. Search bar + results screen — ✅ Done (PR #11)

**What:** Minimal V1 search that powers the home screen's search bar
and the "Because you searched [X]" rail.

- Search bar in the home header opens a full-screen search results
  view.
- Matches on `Tour.title`, `Maker.displayName`, and
  `Tour.primaryCategory` (display name of the category).
- No filters, facets, fuzzy matching, sorting options, or saved
  searches — explicitly deferred per the spec's out-of-scope list.
- Successful queries (i.e., the user opens a tour from the results)
  are appended to local `RecentSearch` history. Cap stored at 20;
  oldest fall off.
- **Place search (2026-06-06, session 23).** A **Places** section above
  the catalog results geocodes the query via Apple's `MKLocalSearch`
  (cities / neighborhoods / landmarks); tapping one flies the Home map
  to that region instead of opening a tour. If the destination has no
  Atlas tours, a transient "No Atlas tours here yet" hint appears on the
  map. Place results are *not* recorded in `RecentSearch` (catalog-only).
  See `PlaceSearchService`, `HomeSharedState.pendingMapMove`,
  `MapRegionGeometry`.

**Files touched:**
- `Features/Search/SearchView.swift` (new — results screen)
- `Features/Search/SearchBar.swift` (new — the pinned bar component
  used in `HomeView`)
- `Data/RecentSearchStore.swift` (new — local persistence for
  `RecentSearch` records)

**How we know it worked:** Tap search bar → results screen opens.
Type "history" → tours with `primaryCategory == .history` appear.
Type a maker name → that maker's tours appear. Tap a result → tour
detail opens → return to home → "Because you searched [X]" rail
now shows the query.

### M-geofencing. GPS-triggered stop playback — ✅ Done (PR #17)

> Always-location decision: shipped with both
> `NSLocationWhenInUseUsageDescription` and
> `NSLocationAlwaysAndWhenInUseUsageDescription` set, so geofences
> can fire while the app is backgrounded / phone locked.

**What:** For multi-stop tours where the maker set stops to
`.geofenced` mode, automatically play the next stop's audio when the
user enters that stop's geofence (default 30m radius). Reuse the
existing `ProximityMonitor` shape with the new `Stop` model. Fire a
local notification when the geofence triggers in the background.

**Files touched:**
- `Location/ProximityMonitor.swift` (rework for stop geofences)
- `Audio/AudioPlayerService.swift` (handle geofence-triggered enqueue)
- `project.pbxproj` (likely needs
  `NSLocationAlwaysAndWhenInUseUsageDescription` for background
  geofencing — owner-facing decision: do we ask for "always" location,
  or accept that geofencing only works while the app is foregrounded?)

**Open question for owner at start of this milestone:** "Always"
location is more powerful (geofences work with the phone locked) but
a heavier permission ask. "When-in-use" works while the player is on
screen but stops when the app backgrounds. Recommended: ask for
when-in-use first; prompt for always-allow only once the user starts
a multi-stop geofenced tour, with copy that explains the tradeoff.

**How we know it worked:** Simulator with simulated location moving
along a tour's stops → audio for stop N plays as the user arrives at
stop N. Phone locked → same behavior + a local notification.

Downloaded with an offline indicator. Settings includes a "Manage

### M-launch-content. Record 5–15 audio tours / pieces

**Owner milestone — not Claude work.** The Atlas team:

- Picks 5–15 locations / themes (mix of single-piece + multi-stop)
- Writes scripts
- Records audio
- Edits and masters
- Captures stop coordinates and trigger preferences
- Uploads audio to the chosen CDN
- Writes maker bios + tour descriptions
- Authors the final `Tours.json` entries

**Authoring scaffold (ready):**
- `docs/authoring-tours.md` — UI-agnostic field-by-field guide
  (every `Tour` / `Stop` / `Maker` field, decision tips, validation
  checklist, quick workflow). Doubles as the spec for the future
  in-app maker upload form.
- `docs/Tours.template.json` — two filled-in example tours
  (single-piece + multi-stop) showing every field. Copy from this
  when authoring real entries.
- `scripts/validate-tours.swift` — pre-commit safety net. Catches
  typos, duplicate UUIDs, broken maker refs, kind ↔ stop count
  mismatches, coord-range errors, audio-duration math problems
  before they crash the app at launch. Run manually with
  `swift scripts/validate-tours.swift` or wire into a git
  pre-commit hook (one-liner in `docs/authoring-tours.md`).

**Hand-off shape:** Once `Tours.json` is updated with real content and
audio URLs resolve, the rest of the app should "just work" — no code
changes required if the prior milestones held to the data contract.

**Audio hosting (decided 2026-05-18):** GitHub Releases for the
design / prototype phase; switch to Cloudflare R2 before public
release. Full reasoning and switch-triggers in `docs/cdn-decision.md`
§ Status. Owner manages Releases uploads; URLs slot into
`Tours.json` like any other HTTPS URL — no code changes required
to use either path.

### M-qa. V1 functionality sanity pass

**What:** End-to-end walkthrough of the running app with real content
loaded. Functional checklist:

1. App launches → map-dominant home with pins, search bar, filter
   chips, and the tour list in the bottom drawer (or graceful
   fallback if no location). The list sorts nearest-first when
   location is granted, with distance labels on each card.
2. Pan the map → the drawer header's "N tours in view" count
   recomputes for the new area.
3. Tap the search bar → results screen works (title / maker /
   category match). Open a tour from search → tour detail opens.
4. Tap a tour (pin, drawer card, or search) → tour detail → tap
   Start → audio plays.
5. Lock the phone → audio continues; lock-screen controls work.
6. Multi-stop geofenced tour → simulated walking along stops → next
   stop's audio triggers on arrival.
7. Multi-stop manual tour → tap next stop in player → its audio plays.
8. Download a tour → airplane mode → tour plays end to end.
9. Save a tour → force-quit + relaunch → still saved.
10. Maker page → tour list correct → tap tour → tour detail opens.

**M-qa device pass — build 1.0 (4) (2026-05-21).** First on-device
walkthrough. Checklist 1, 3, 4, 5, 8, 9, 10 passed. Items 6/7
(multi-stop geofenced + manual) deferred — no multi-stop tour is in
the current catalog. Five UX issues surfaced and were fixed the same
session (remote — code on the feature branch, ships to device on the
next TestFlight build):

- **Mini-player added.** A persistent now-playing bar sits between
  the home drawer and the tab bar whenever audio is loaded —
  pause/resume inline, tap to reopen the full player. New
  `Features/Player/MiniPlayerBar.swift`; hosted by `ContentView`;
  the home drawer's peek height grows to clear it.
- **Hero-image bleed-through at the peek detent fixed** — the
  drawer's scroll list now fades out at peek instead of leaking a
  sliver of the first card above the tab bar.
- **Recenter animation** — the camera now glides to the user
  (0.45 s ease) instead of snapping; the recenter button's
  fade-in/out on map pan is gentler.
- **User-location dot rebuilt** — explicit Apple-Maps blue, plus a
  directional heading wedge driven by the device compass
  (`LocationManager.heading`, iOS only).

**M-qa device pass — build 1.0 (5) (2026-05-22).** Full walkthrough
on device. Checklist items 1–5 and 8–10 all passed, plus every
build-5 UX change verified — the always-present mini-player (idle +
active states), the square-topped island, the tour-detail action
bar, drawer-card carousels, pinch-to-zoom hero images, the compass
heading wedge, the Library/Settings island backgrounds, and
drawer-detent persistence across tab switches. Items 6/7 (multi-stop
geofenced + manual) still deferred — no multi-stop tour exists.
**No issues found.** V1 functionality is device-validated; only the
multi-stop checks remain, blocked on content.

**Files touched:** none expected. Bugs become small targeted fixes.

**Outcome:** V1 ready for owner review via TestFlight, or for the
polish phase below.

**Pre-QA self-audit (2026-05-18).** Before M-qa actually runs on
device, a code self-audit (landed via PR #21; doc archived to
`archive/pre-qa-audit-260518.md` once most P0s closed) surfaced
22 findings — bugs, fragile edges, accessibility gaps, polish —
categorized P0 (launch blockers) → P3 (polish/debt). Running
status; check items off as fixes land:

**P0 — Launch blockers**
- [x] P0-1. Home → Tour Detail navigation silently broken (PR #23)
- [x] P0-2. Dark Mode catastrophically broken (PR #22)
- [x] P0-3. Typography hierarchy collapsed (PR #22)
- [x] P0-4. SettingsView 23 hardcoded `.foregroundStyle(.black)` (PR #22)
- [x] P0-5. Geofenced playback fails offline for downloaded tours (PR #24)
- [x] P0-6. Geofence notifications invisible in foreground (PR #24)
- [/] P0-7. Developer-facing copy in user UI — HomeView + LibraryView fixed in PR #23. SettingsView debug-counts row deferred to a follow-up after PR #22.

**P1 — Bugs**
- [x] P1-1. "Continue listening" / "Recently played" sort by wrong field (lastListenedAt added)
- [x] P1-2. Maker avatar URL is ignored (AsyncImage with circle fallback)
- [x] P1-3. Player-tour identification by title is fragile (currentSourceId on AudioPlayerService)
- [x] P1-4. HeroImageView doesn't load remote images (AsyncImage with placeholder fallback)
- [x] P1-5. Audio session interruption (phone call) not handled (PR #24)
- [x] P1-6. Headphone unplug doesn't pause audio (PR #24)
- [x] P1-7. International-dateline bug in coordinate-in-region check (MKCoordinateRegion.contains extension)

**P2 — Accessibility**
- [x] P2-1. BottomSheet has no VoiceOver affordance (label + accessibilityAdjustableAction)
- [/] P2-2. Map preview close button sub-44pt touch target — obsolete; the preview-card-with-X pattern was removed in the PR #19 home redesign
- [x] P2-3. Download button disabled state not announced (label + hint when isOtherActive)
- [x] P2-4. No "Open Settings" deep link when location denied (button in SettingsView, iOS/visionOS)
- [x] P2-5. Localization gap — duration / distance formatters hardcoded English/metric (new AtlasFormatters)

**P3 — Polish & tech debt** (ten items; see audit doc; none block V1)
- [ ] P3-1. Hardcoded values bypass theme-tokens — deferred with the design pass
- [x] P3-2. `formattedDuration` duplicated — closed incidentally by P2-5's `AtlasFormatters`
- [ ] P3-3. O(n) lookups everywhere — premature; V1 has 12 tours
- [x] P3-4. TourDownloader no retry on transient failures (3 retries, exponential backoff 1s/2s/4s, transient errors only — terminal failures still fail immediately)
- [ ] P3-5. No tour-completed UX
- [ ] P3-6. Splash screen bare-bones — deferred with the polish pass
- [x] P3-7. ContentView calls `requestPermission` on every appearance (guarded by `didRequestLocationPermission` flag)
- [x] P3-8. Search doesn't index tags or descriptions (added tag + description buckets with rank ordering)
- [ ] P3-9. No delete swipe in Library
- [x] P3-10. ManageDownloadsView ordering undefined (sorted alphabetically by title)

**Lifecycle.** The audit doc itself was archived on 2026-05-18
after the P0 wave landed (PRs #22 / #23 / #24). The closed PRs
are the authoritative record of fixes; this checklist is the
live "what's left." Remaining P1s will batch into a cleanup PR
before M-qa runs.

| Milestone | Scope |

Runs locally via Cmd-U and on CI per PR. Cadence rule: see `CLAUDE.md` § "When to run tests." |
